\mathcal{K}\rightarrow\mathscr{K}
and the fact that
\mathrm{tr}\mathcal{K}=1
. The fact that

\mathscr{K}
is symmetric with respect to
\bar{h}
is an immediate consequence of the fact that there is a frame of eigenvector fields of
\mathscr{K}
which is
orthonormal with respect to
\bar{h}
. The relation
\mathrm{tr}\mathscr{K}^{2}+\bar{\Phi}_{a}^{2}=1
holds by assumption. That
(
55a
) holds is an immediate consequence of (
155
), (
163
),
(
44b
) and the definition of
\check{h}
; cf. (
54
). That (
55b
) holds
is an immediate consequence of (
53
). What remains to be demonstrated is that

\mathrm{div}_{\bar{h}}\mathscr{K}=\bar{\Phi}_{a}d\bar{\Phi}_{b}
. The template for doing so is the proof of Lemma
82
. However,
the definition of
X_{A}
etc. used in this proof differs from the definition of the present section. Let
\bar{X}_{A}=e^{-\bar{r}_{A}}X_{A}
(no summation)
and define
e^{\check{\mu}_{A}}
by
|\bar{X}_{A}|_{\bar{g}}=e^{\check{\mu}_{A}}
. Then
\{\bar{X}_{A}\}
is a
C^{k}
-frame with dual
C^{k}
frame
\{\bar{Y}^{A}\}
. Note also that

\{\bar{\mathscr{X}}_{A}\}
satisfies the conditions stated in Remark
11
, except for the regularity; the vector fields

\bar{\mathscr{X}}_{A}
are
C^{k}
. Next, since
\bar{r}_{A}=r_{A}+p_{A}r_{\theta}
,

\begin{split}\check{\mu}_{A}+p_{A}\ln\theta=&\bar{\mu}_{A}-\bar{r}_{A}+p_{A}\ln\theta=(\bar{\mu}_{A}-p_{A}\varrho-r_{A})+p_{A}\varrho+r_{A}-r_{A}-p_{A}r_{\theta}+p_{A}\ln\theta\\
=&(\bar{\mu}_{A}-p_{A}\varrho-r_{A})+p_{A}(\varrho+\ln\theta-r_{\theta}).\end{split}

Combining this equality with (
161
) and (
163
) yields

|\bar{D}^{l}(\check{\mu}_{A}+p_{A}\ln\theta)|_{\bar{g}_{\mathrm{ref}}}\leq C_{k}\langle\varrho\rangle^{a_{k}}e^{2\epsilon\varrho}

on
M
for all
A
and all
0\leq l\leq k
. Given the above estimates, we can revisit the proof of
Lemma
82
, with suitable substitutions:
X_{A}
and
Y^{A}
are replaced by
\bar{X}_{A}
and
\bar{Y}^{A}

respectively;
\bar{\mu}_{A}
is replaced by
\check{\mu}_{A}
;
\Phi_{1}
and
\Phi_{0}
are replaced by
\bar{\Phi}_{a}
and
\bar{\Phi}_{b}
respectively; the
structure constants are calculated with respect to
\bar{X}_{A}
etc. The conclusion is that
\mathrm{div}_{\bar{h}}\mathscr{K}=\bar{\Phi}_{a}d\bar{\Phi}_{b}
. The
theorem follows.
∎

Finally, we are in a position to prove Theorem
51
.

Theorem
51
.

Note that the conditions of Theorems
46
and
49
are fulfilled. In particular,
it thus follows that the metric can be represented as in (
49a
). The corresponding
\check{h}
is given by

\check{h}:=\textstyle{\sum}_{A,B}c_{AB}\mathscr{Y}^{A}\otimes\mathscr{Y}^{B}.

Due to (
50
), it follows that the limit of
\check{h}
is the
\bar{h}
appearing in the statement of Theorem
49
; in fact,

\textstyle{\sum}_{l=0}^{k}|\bar{D}^{l}(\hat{h}-\bar{h})|_{\bar{g}_{\mathrm{ref}}}\leq C_{k}\langle\ln t\rangle^{a_{k}}t^{2\epsilon}.

Moreover, the limit of
\mathcal{K}
, i.e.
\mathscr{K}
, is of course the same. This means that
\mathrm{div}_{\bar{h}}\mathscr{K}=0
, that
\mathrm{tr}\mathscr{K}^{2}=1
,
\mathrm{tr}\mathscr{K}=1
and that

\mathscr{K}
is symmetric with respect to
\bar{h}
. Moreover,
\mathscr{Y}^{1}[\mathscr{X}_{2},\mathscr{X}_{3}]=0
. Assuming
\mathscr{K}
to be
C^{\infty}
,
Lemma
55

yields coordinates
(V,\bar{\mathsf{x}})
such that the conclusions of Lemma
55
hold; denote the renormalised bases by

\{\hat{\mathscr{X}}_{A}\}
and
\{\hat{\mathscr{Y}}^{A}\}
. Then
\mathscr{Y}^{A}=\beta_{A}\hat{\mathscr{Y}}^{A}
(no summation) for some positive functions
\beta_{A}
. Moreover,

\begin{split}\textstyle{\sum}_{A,B}c_{AB}t^{2p_{\max\{A,B\}}}\mathscr{Y}^{A}\otimes\mathscr{Y}^{B}=&\textstyle{\sum}_{A,B}\bar{c}_{AB}t^{2p_{\max\{A,B\}}}\hat{\mathscr{Y}}^{A}\otimes\hat{\mathscr{Y}}^{B}\\
=&\textstyle{\sum}_{i,j}d_{ij}t^{2p_{\max\{i,j\}}}d\bar{\mathsf{x}}^{i}\otimes d\bar{\mathsf{x}}^{j},\end{split}

where
\bar{c}_{AB}=\beta_{A}c_{AB}\beta_{B}
(no summation),

\textstyle{\sum}_{l=0}^{k}|\bar{D}^{l}(\bar{c}_{AB}-\bar{c}_{A}^{2}\delta_{AB})|\leq C\langle\ln t\rangle^{a_{k}}t^{2\epsilon}

and
\bar{c}_{A}=\beta_{A}e^{\bar{r}_{A}}
(no summation); cf. (
49b
). Moreover, it can be deduced that
(
56
) holds on
W
, where
\bar{d}_{ii}=\bar{c}_{i}^{2}>0
. Combining this observation with Lemma
55
yields
(
57
). Next, note that (
46d
) can be written

(178)

\textstyle{\sum}_{l=0}^{k}|\bar{D}^{l}[\mathcal{K}(\mathscr{Y}^{A},\mathscr{X}_{B})-\mathscr{K}(\mathscr{Y}^{A},\mathscr{X}_{B})]|\leq C_{k}\langle\ln t\rangle^{a_{k}}t^{2\epsilon}\min\{1,t^{2(p_{B}-p_{A})}\}.

One immediate consequence of this estimate is that

\textstyle{\sum}_{l=0}^{k}|\bar{D}^{l}[\mathcal{K}^{i}_{j}-\mathscr{K}^{i}_{j}]|_{\bar{g}_{\mathrm{ref}}}\leq C_{k}\langle\ln t\rangle^{a_{k}}t^{2\epsilon}

on
W
for
i\geq j
, where
\mathcal{K}^{i}_{j}=\mathcal{K}(d\bar{\mathsf{x}}^{i},\partial_{j})
. On the other hand,

\mathcal{K}(d\bar{\mathsf{x}}^{1},\partial_{2})=\mathcal{K}(\hat{\mathscr{Y}}^{1},\hat{\mathscr{X}}_{2}-\hat{\mathscr{X}}^{3}_{2}\hat{\mathscr{X}}_{3})=O_{k}(\langle\ln t\rangle^{a_{k}}t^{2(p_{2}-p_{1}+\epsilon)})

on
W
, where we appealed to (
178
) and Lemma
55
, and the notation
O_{k}

signifies that the relevant estimates hold for up to
k
derivatives. The arguments concerning the remaining components are similar and
the estimate

(179)

\textstyle{\sum}_{l=0}^{k}|\bar{D}^{l}[\mathcal{K}^{i}_{j}-\mathscr{K}^{i}_{j}]|_{\bar{g}_{\mathrm{ref}}}\leq C_{k}\langle\ln t\rangle^{a_{k}}t^{2\epsilon}\min\{1,t^{2(p_{j}-p_{i})}\}

follows (on
W
). By a similar argument, appealing to (
46e
),

(180)

\textstyle{\sum}_{l=0}^{k}|\bar{D}^{l}[(\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{U}\mathcal{K})^{i}_{j}]|\leq C_{k}\langle\ln t\rangle^{a_{k}}t^{2\epsilon}\min\{1,t^{2(p_{j}-p_{i})}\}

on
W
. Combining these estimates with (
48
), it can be deduced that (
58
) holds.

Finally, combining the fact that
\mathrm{div}_{\bar{h}}\mathscr{K}=0
with the arguments presented in the proof of Lemma
59
yields the
final conclusion of the lemma.
∎
